Report: Surprise €10m deal tabled for Liverpool veteran

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Liverpool have a lot of decisions that they need to make on their squad over the next few months.

A handful of their first-team squad all have their contracts expiring in June 2027 and, with no negotiations lined up for any of them, another massive overhaul could be just around the corner.

Of course, the number of transfers that Liverpool have been linked with is obscene but, at least it proves that the Reds are casting their net far and wide for their next transfers.

Some positions are obviously more in need of reinforcement than others but, since Liverpool could end up moving some of their own players on as well, nobody actually knows what the next year or so could look like for the Anfield outfit.

That is, especially when multi-million deals are put up for grabs.

AC Milan want to offer Virgil van Dijk €10 million per year to join them from Liverpool

Virgil van Dijk has easily been one of Liverpool's best players ever since he joined.

No matter who he's been partnered with or who the Reds have faced, the Dutchman has always managed to come out as one of the stand-out performers.

Indeed, things have gone a bit wrong for him every now and then but, more often than not, he's proven why he's one of the world's best centre-backs.

However, now that he's 35 years old, questions are being asked about how long he can keep these performance levels up for.

Liverpool have already started to line up players such as Giovanni Leoni and Jeremy Jacquet to replace him but, right now, Andoni Iraola's men would be lost without their captain in the starting XI.

So, that's why the latest update from MilanNews24 is so surprising.

"According to what MilanNews24 has learned, Cardinale would like to make a concrete attempt," they wrote.

"It's not just a tempting idea or a name linked to AC Milan to fuel the fans' ambitions: the Rossoneri owner is reportedly willing to seriously evaluate the conditions for bringing the Dutchman to Italy.

"To land Van Dijk, there are two key conditions. The first concerns Champions League qualification , a crucial element in presenting the defender with an ambitious and competitive sporting project.

"The second is naturally economic: a two-year contract worth 8-10 million euros per season would be on the table .

"A significant figure, which testifies to the scope of the investment envisioned by Cardinale.

"AC Milan is therefore dreaming of an international signing for the summer of 2027. Van Dijk would be a signing capable of bringing experience, leadership, and personality. Now they need to turn that dream into a concrete possibility."

Virgil van Dijk is still one of Liverpool's most important players

As mentioned, Van Dijk is key to the way that Liverpool play at the moment.

When he's left on the bench for a cup tie or a less important game, the difference in quality is staggering.

The Dutchman may not seem like he does all that much when he's on the pitch but, in reality, he's the glue that holds the team together.

Of course, other players such as Ronald Araujo or Joe Gomez can try and plug the gap left by the Dutchman but, neither of them are anywhere near his level.

It was always going to be a worry that Van Dijk would leave for free at the end of this season but, the fact that a concrete bid is about to come from AC Milan is surprising.

Most reports that emerge from Italy make it clear that most Serie A clubs are skint with several outfits often linked with haggling Liverpool for a discount on Federico Chiesa's impending transfer.

So, the fact that AC Milan are willing to give Van Dijk around €10 million per year is shocking.

His stature and name in the game will obviously carry a lot of weight but, unless you're a team who knows you can afford his wages such as Liverpool, it feels a tad strange for him to be targeted by anyone else.

Investors brace for lower Anthropic valuation, calling $1.5 trillion a 'stretch'

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Following tech's selloff on Wall Street this week, some investors are lowering their expectations for Anthropic.

The artificial intelligence company had been looking toward a $2 trillion valuation in its IPO up until recently, but stakeholders told CNBC that even $1.5 trillion is now in doubt, with one investor calling that number a "stretch."

The change in sentiment comes alongside growing concerns about the outsized spending by AI companies and a sharp post-midterm slump in the market. The Nasdaq plummeted more than 5% on Thursday, its steepest drop in 19 months, reflecting fears that Democrats' newfound power in Washington, D.C., and a nationwide rebuke of AI will lead to a slowdown in the buildout of data centers.

Bearishness on the AI trade was on the rise before the election. The Nasdaq suffered its worst month in October since March 2025, led by declines in chipmakers and neoclouds.

Anthropic is set to list on the Nasdaq, under the ticker ANT, and is gearing up for an expected debut in a matter of weeks. Last month, Anthropic was reportedly seeking a valuation as high as $2 trillion. As of May, the company was privately valued at $965 billion.

With the latest market turmoil, one investor said $1 trillion to $1.25 trillion is a more likely valuation target, although that could change with market conditions. The investors asked not to be named because they're not authorized to speak about the matter.

Gil Luria, an analyst at D.A. Davidson, told CNBC that, "Investors will expect the IPO to be priced at least at the low end of previous expectations."

"The market really needs this IPO to go well and the bankers on the deal surely understand that," Luria said. "The best way to set Anthropic up for success is to price the deal well."

The company is expected to kick off its roadshow next week.

What's that bright 'star' in the early morning sky? It's not a star at all!

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Spotted a dazzling light in the eastern sky before sunrise? It's Venus, and the planet is only going to get brighter as it heads toward its spectacular peak later this month.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Early morning risers heading to work or school before sunrise are likely to notice a very bright, star-like object appearing low in the east-southeast sky just before sunrise. What is somewhat amazing is that just a couple of weeks ago this object wasn't there at all, but now it literally stands out with such brilliance as to almost command the attention of commuters who might be traveling in an easterly direction to "Look at me!"

The object in question is, of course, the planet Venus, which is currently amid one of its most spectacular forays into the morning sky, literally "vaulting up" into prominence.

On Oct. 24, Venus came to inferior conjunction, passing between the Earth and sun and shifting from the evening into the morning sky. And the view is only going to get better in the days and weeks to come!

In terms of morning visibility, Venus is now in a literal and figurative sense "rising to the occasion." It's now rising before the break of dawn, more than 90 minutes before sunrise. From here on, it will rise three minutes earlier each day, so that by Dec. 17 it will rise 3 hours and 50 minutes before sunrise and blaze in a completely dark sky for more than two hours before the first vestige of morning twilight.

It will reach peak brilliance on Nov. 29, when it will shine at an eye-popping magnitude of -4.9. That's 12 times brighter than its nearest planetary competitor, Jupiter and a full 25 times more dazzling than Sirius, the brightest of all stars. If you live in a rural location with no light pollution to interfere, you might be able to make out that it is casting a visible shadow and if you follow it right on through sunrise, it will be evident to your naked eye in full daylight: a tiny white speck plainly evident against the blue daytime sky.

In a way, we could even regard Venus as this year's version of a "Christmas Star," glowing with stunning radiance ". . . in the east" like the biblical quote mentioned in St. Matthew.

After reaching its high point in terms of morning visibility in mid-December, Venus will start a long, slow slide back toward the sun, rising about 1½ minutes later each morning from New Year's Day through late March. By then, Venus will be coming up as dawn is breaking, roughly 100 minutes before sunrise. Venus will continue to get gradually lower and rise progressively later during the spring and by late June it will be coming up less than an hour before sunrise, ultimately vanishing into the bright dawn twilight by July. Venus will then go on a "summer sabbatical" of sorts and will not reappear again until about this time next year when it will begin a very fine evening apparition.

So, if any of your friends or relatives remark to you that they saw a "strange UFO" on the way to work or school recently, you now can set them straight.

A farmer in Mexico saw the ground split open in his cornfield, then watched a volcano grow to 1,391 feet tall

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

In 1943, Mexico's Parícutin volcano erupted from a farmer's cornfield, growing rapidly and burying two villages during nine years of activity.

On February 20, 1943, farmer Dionisio Pulido watched a fissure open in his field near the village of Parícutin, in the Mexican state of Michoacán. Smoke rose from the crack, along with a hissing sound and a smell of sulfur.

Within a week, the new cone stood more than 490 feet (150 meters) tall. When the eruption ended in 1952, it had risen 1,391 feet (424 meters) above the cornfield where it began.

Lava buried two villages along the way. Parícutin was the first volcano whose eruption modern scientists observed in detail from its birth, according to the International Union of Geological Sciences (IUGS).

Starting around February 5 to 7, 1943, local witnesses felt about two weeks of earthquake activity before the eruption.

Felipe Cuara Amezcua, then municipal president of San Juan Parangaricutiro, later reported that the tremors grew until more than 200 shook the area in a single day.

On February 20, Pulido went to his farm to prepare the fields for the spring sowing. He had owned the land for 31 years.

Smithsonian mineralogist William F. Foshag and Mexican geologist Jenaro González R. later collected his account and his wife’s. A 1956 U.S. Geological Survey bulletin printed them in English translation.

At about 4 p.m., Pulido left his wife, Paula, and went to burn a pile of branches. She had been hearing underground noise and thunder.

“About 4 p. m., after talking to my husband, I heard a kind of loud whistle,” Paula Pulido recalled.

“I saw that it was a kind of fissure that had only a depth of half a meter,” Pulido said.

He then saw the ground swell by about 6.6 to 8 feet (2 to 2.5 meters), while gray smoke or dust rose from the crack.

“Immediately more smoke began to rise, with a hiss or whistle, loud and continuous,” he recounted.

Frightened, he mounted his mare and galloped to the village where his family was waiting.

Accounts from Pulido and other witnesses gave “the first adequate account of the birth of a new volcano,” Foshag and González wrote.

One of the first photographs of the eruption, taken at 6 p.m. on February 20, shows an ash plume rising from the new vent. By midnight, the cone was about 20 feet (6 meters) high.

On the morning of February 21, a strong earthquake caused panic. People from Parícutin fled to San Juan Parangaricutiro, and many from San Juan went on to Angahuan or Uruapan.

A photograph from February 21 shows the cone about 100 feet (30 meters) tall, just rising above the treetops. After six days of activity, it reached 548 feet (167 meters).

The first lava flow, called the Quitzocho flow, moved north over cornfields prepared for planting. Mexican geologist Ezequiel Ordóñez estimated that the early lava advanced about 10 feet (3 meters) an hour.

Ash fell too. It piled up deeper than 6 inches (15 centimeters) over an area of 116 square miles (300 square kilometers) around the volcano.

When the first phase of activity ended on October 19, 1943, the original vent had built a cone about 1,200 feet (365 meters) high.

Pulido’s home village lay only 1.9 miles (3 kilometers) northwest of the new cone. Its 733 residents had to abandon it for good four months after the eruption began, and lava later flowed over the site.

Starting in January 1944, a new phase sent out the Taquí lava flows, which spread over about 7 square miles (18 square kilometers) by 1945.

In July 1944, lava surrounded the church of San Juan Parangaricutiro. Its tower still stands in the middle of a lava flow, the IUGS notes.

In all, the volcano’s lavas reached up to 2.5 miles (4 kilometers) from the vent and formed a field of about 9.7 square miles (25 square kilometers).

The Mexican government gave displaced residents new land in Caltzontzín, 17 miles (27 kilometers) to the southeast.

Parícutin was “the first well-documented example of an eruption of a monogenetic volcanic cone in historical time,” according to a 1993 USGS film report.

A monogenetic cone forms during a single eruptive episode. Parícutin, active for about nine years, gave scientists an unprecedented chance to follow such a volcano through its full life cycle.

An international group documented the activity in real time. Celedonio Gutiérrez, a resident of San Juan who kept a diary from the first day, worked with U.S. scientists throughout the eruption.

In 1990, volcanologists I. Yokoyama and S. de la Cruz-Reyna analyzed the precursory earthquakes of the 1943 eruption, a type of signal that comes before eruptions.

In 1985, decades after the eruption stopped, a fumarole at Parícutin still measured 883°F (473°C). The cone is one of more than 1,400 vents in the Michoacán-Guanajuato volcanic field.

The information was obtained from the Smithsonian Institution’s Global Volcanism Program.

‘To Hold a Mountain’ Took on the Government — Now It’s Montenegro’s Oscar Submission

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

For one of the most important screenings of “To Hold a Mountain,” the filmmakers had to bring their own electricity. In July, co-directors Biljana Tutorov and Petar Glomazić hauled a tent, a generator, and a DCP up to the Montenegrin highlands where they had spent years filming.

The creative couple, also partnered in life, wanted the shepherds who lived and worked in Sinjajevina to see the movie they had made possible. But by then, Montenegro had already resolved their story’s central conflict — scrapping the government’s plan to turn these people’s ancestral pastures into a military training ground after joining NATO in 2017. Related Stories ‘Soul Patrol’ Review: The Vietnam War’s First Black Special Ops Team Shares Their Stories in An Enlightening Documentary Could ‘The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth’ Re-Ignite Quentin Tarantino’s Bruce Lee Controversy?

“To Hold a Mountain” won the World Cinema Grand Jury Prize for documentary at Sundance, and Montenegro’s prime minister and president even showed up to the shepherds’ screening with their families, unannounced. When the film opens at New York’s Film Forum on December 16, American audiences will see the feature’s updated conclusion.

“We reopened the film to continue shooting and then a second end happened that was very empowering and powerful,” Tutorov said at IndieWire’s Art of the Doc series.

During our conversation, the filmmaker recalled arriving in Sinjajevina with Glomazić on the first day of protest. They wanted to help the community and make a movie, and agreed early on that those were two different jobs.

“We will separate our activist mission from our filmmaker’s mission,” she said. “That was a very good decision because it allowed us to be very bold in both roles.”

Onscreen, the fight over who would get to lay claim to the mountain centered on two people who already did: Gara, a shepherd and community leader, and Nada, the young girl she was raising. As the threat to their home brought a history of violence in their own family to the surface, the directors had to reckon with how much of Nada’s private life might become public.

“We really wanted it to be an empowering experience for [her],” Tutorov said.

Nada was twelve and a half when the filmmakers began to understand what the story would ask of her, and Tutorov said they decided to keep filming until she turned eighteen — precisely so Nada could decide for herself whether she wanted to be part of it. The crew logged more than 230 shooting days, though some yielded only half an hour of footage.

Gara and Nada had work to do, too. The directors and cinematographer Eva Kraljević shared a small shepherd’s shed, assisting with various chores including cows that had to be milked twice a day. Over successive summers, their routine became a framing device. Tutorov, who studied art history, thinks of the mountain as a kind of chorus bearing witness to living things on it.

Last July, just as the filmmakers were wrapping up, Montenegro’s government canceled the training-ground plan. Tutorov believes “To Hold a Mountain” and how it told the existing community’s story influenced that decision. She also pointed out that a different government was in power by 2025, following years of organizing by Sinjajevina locals.

At the shepherd’s screening a year later, Prime Minister Milojko Spajić said the film reinforced his view that canceling the plan had been the right call. Then Montenegro chose “To Hold a Mountain” as its official submission for the international feature Oscar. Tutorov said there was irony in realizing that, after years of fighting the government, she and Glomazić would be proudly representing the country on a global stage.

“It feels like they really kind of destroyed our strategy,” she said, warmly.

It’s a good joke, and an even better reason to remember that rough-and-tumble showing. For an evening, the people who’d spent years making their case to the government got to host its leaders on the very land they’d fought to keep. Tutorov recalled what Montenegro’s president told the filmmakers after seeing the movie: “Thank you guys for teaching us democracy.”

National Geographic Documentary Films, part of a joint venture between The Walt Disney Company and the National Geographic Society, is committed to bringing the world premium, feature documentaries that cover timely, provocative and globally relevant stories from the very best documentary filmmakers. Its award-winning and critically acclaimed films reach 300 million people worldwide in 180 countries and 33 languages across the global National Geographic channels and direct-to-consumer platforms Disney+ and Hulu.Recent films include the Emmy-nominated winner of the IDA Award for Best Feature “The Tale of Silyan;” Oscar® and Peabody nominee “Sugarcane;” Oscar-nominated and Peabody Award winner “Bobi Wine: The People’s President;” Emmy Award winner “Lost In The Jungle;” Oscar- and BAFTA-nominated “Fire of Love;” Emmy and Peabody Award winner “The Territory;” BAFTA nominees “The Rescue” and “Becoming Cousteau;” and Oscar- and BAFTA winner “Free Solo.” For more information visit films.nationalgeographic.com, or find us onFacebook,Twitter,Instagram, and YouTube.

President Trump Moves to Make Oval Office a Public Monument

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

President Donald Trump signed a proclamation Thursday seeking to designate the Oval Office and the adjacent Cabinet Room national monuments, saying that the new designation would prevent future administrations from making major modifications without Congressional approval. In his second term, Trump made it a priority to redecorate both, turning the previously restrained presidential working spaces into extravagantly gilded interiors in the style of Louis XIV.

In the wake of this week’s midterm elections, in which Republicans suffered historic defeats, Trump appears to be self-soothing by focusing on the many renovation projects he has initiated at the White House and across Washington, D.C. At the White House, in addition to his makeover of the Oval Office, these have included the construction of a granite helipad on the White House’s South Lawn and, most notoriously, the demolition of the building’s East Wing to make way for a 90,000-square-foot, $600 million ballroom. (On Wednesday, in his first public appearance after Tuesday’s Republican wipeout, Trump led a media tour of the ballroom construction site.) Related Articles After a Democratic Victory in Midterm Elections, Trump Focuses on Touting His Ballroom: 'This Is a Special Structure' In Fiery Court Filing, Democratic Lawmaker Says Kennedy Center's Closure Is Meant to Hide Its Failure Under Trump

Around the nation’s capital, Trump has embarked on a fraught, $14 million repair of the Lincoln Memorial Reflecting Pool, begun the redevelopment of a popular public golf course by cutting down hundreds of trees, and proposed a 250-foot-high triumphal arch near Arlington National Cemetery. Apparently worried that incoming Democrats would block construction of the arch, Trump asked for hastened approval of the project, which was granted Wednesday by his hand-picked planning commission; that evening, Judge Tanya Chutkan stopped construction for fourteen days.

It is not clear how designating the Oval Office a national monument will prevent another president from simply revising, or ignoring, the designation—the White House is already designated a National Historic Landmark, which has not stopped Trump from building his ballroom. Nevertheless, speaking from the now gold-saturated Oval Office, Trump told reporters, “When completed, and when we get the documentation fully done, legally binding, it’ll make it impossible for anybody ever to destroy this incredible space.”

Chainalysis says hackers stole credentials and tokens for all of its customers

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Company said a “multifront cyberattack” included AI agents and an on-path data snatch.

Blockchain analysis firm Chainalysis said that hackers have obtained sensitive personal information—including names, usernames, password hashes, and live tokens—for all of its users following a breach of its network security.

The accomplishment could be a major coup for the attackers. Both Chainalysis and public sources have revealed a long list of high-profile customers, including government agencies such as the FBI, US Immigration and Customs Enforcement agency, and the US Air Force, as well as private companies such as Microsoft, Coinbase, and Barclays. It’s unclear if the data theft exposed customers to additional threats.

Last Friday, Chainalysis said it had been the target of a cybersecurity incident in October that was similar to the hacks of other platforms during the same month. The external actor used AI agent-assisted offensive techniques to gain unauthorized access to Chainalysis systems and internal tools. An email the company sent to affected customers said some “non-sensitive customer data, such as user IDs, full names, email addresses, and organization role labels for users provisioned on our platform were accessed.” It’s not clear why the company considered such information “non-sensitive.”

In a Wednesday update, the company said that part of the “ongoing multifront cyberattack” involved an “on-path attack” that exposed a subset of customer passwords, and that the attack vector had been identified and fixed. Also known as an adversary-in-the-middle attack, on-path attacks happen when a hacker gains access to a piece of infrastructure sending traffic back and forth between two parties. Chainalysis has yet to say where the infrastructure compromise occurred and if the data obtained from it was TLS encrypted.

The company said later that day that an investigation two days earlier (on Monday) had revealed that “certain customer information including names, usernames, password hashes and live tokens for all Chainalysis users were exposed to a malicious actor.” It also said it had detected “abnormal activity” and, in response, took down its login page and know-your-transaction service, which monitors threats to cryptocurrency holdings in real time. The company has also removed access to its Reactor and Data Solutions services.

In an update on Friday, Chainalysis said it had begun phasing in customer access to KYT and Address Screening and expected more customers to follow. It asked customers to submit their trusted IP addresses so they can be added to an allow list and to prepare to reset credentials once they regain access. The credential rotation is mandatory.

The company says its investigation has yet to reveal any evidence that any investigation data has been accessed or Chainalysis attribution data has been manipulated.

Several important questions remain unanswered. As noted, the source that made the on-path attack possible is still unknown. It’s not clear how useful the information obtained in such an attack would be unless the attackers also had the ability to decrypt the traffic they were monitoring. If that were the case, the attackers might have gained broad control of the company’s infrastructure.

Chainalysis also didn’t explain why it waited until Wednesday to disclose the data theft of all customers’ personal information when its investigation discovered the exposure two days earlier. Further, the company has provided no details about the AI agents used in the attack and how they worked.

A Chainalysis representative said the company wasn’t commenting beyond the statements in its blog post.

After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

The vibrant community behind the popular memecoin just revived the $1 debate: are burns the main catalyst?

Shiba Inu’s (SHIB) official X account just quoted a rather optimistic outlook on the popular meme coin’s path forward, assessing the frequently-discussed $1 price target. Shiba Inu’s (SHIB) price tends to swing harder than the major-cap altcoins like Solana (SOL) or Ethereum (ETH) due to the speculative nature of the token.

A few days back, a crypto currency comparison between the major-caps surfaced online, and SHIB was included. In the insight, it was established that Shiba Inu (SHIB) “acts as a high-beta asset”. The analyst called it trading way below its potential, struggling due to the 589 trillion supply that makes a $1 tag “mathematically improbable, but NOT IMPOSSIBLE.”

The comparison table shows a $0.000012 price on the popular Japanese guard dog-themed coin. Realistically, these levels haven’t been recouped in 2026 yet.

No doubt, the big question is how to erase the 589 trillion SHIB tokens that are in circulation. At $1 each, the token’s market cap would reach about $589 trillion. That figure is simple arithmetic, price multiplied by supply, not the amount of money buyers would need to put in. Notably, this figure has considerably dropped from the initial 999 trillion due to community burns.

Presently, beyond 41% of the total supply has been torched. The regular SHIB burning by separate individuals and community initiatives has pushed SHIB’s weekly burn rate up by 190.45%, but it still revolves around millions of tokens rather than billions burned. Gradually, the stats could pick up along with the usage of Shibarium, the community’s native Layer-2 chain.

How to Judge Trophy Pronghorn in the Field

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Slipping up to the crest of the sage-flecked knoll, we peered down at a pair of stunning tan-and-white pronghorns. “What a buck,” I said, eyeing the closer one. “He’ll go close to 15 inches.”

“Yep,” my guide replied. “Now, if he’ll move a bit, we’ll get a better look at the real trophy behind him.” Making a classic novice’s mistake, I had assumed the taller buck closest to us was superior.

No sight is more emblematic of Western hunting than a pronghorn buck with jet-black horns staring back at you, poised to prance across the prairie at 70 mph. But just how good of a trophy is he? How thick and impressive will those horns be when you wrap your hands around them? Pronghorns are notoriously difficult to judge in the field. So I spoke to three veteran guides who have a century of analyzing antelope horns among them: Bill Ferranti of New Mexico, Bob Daugherty of Colorado, and Pete Dube of Wyoming.

Most hunters focus on this. A 12-inch antelope is average; 13, good; 14, exceptional. Record-book pronghorns often tape 15 to 16 inches. But horns that jut straight up appear longer than they really are, while those curling back and inward, or with ivory tips, ­appear shorter.

The Solution: Try to view the buck from multiple angles so you can add the curl of the horn tips to your length estimate.

Tip: Use the distance between the tip of the animal’s nose and its eye (about 8 inches) or between its ears (12 inches) as a guide to horn length. Also look for a buck whose prongs protrude from the horn an inch or more above its ears.

These are measured from the tip of the prong, along its upper edge, then across to the back center of the horn: 3 to 4 inches is average; 4 to 5, good; 5 to 6, excellent; and 6 to 7, ­record book. What protrudes from the horn is only part of the measurement. You need to figure out how many more inches you gain across the horn to its back axis.

The solution: Judge how much prong actually protrudes from the horn and then add about 2 inches for the width of the horn itself. A prong that extends 3 inches is excellent once you add those couple of inches.

Tip: Use the length of the ear, about 6 inches, for ­comparison. If the full prong measurement is almost that long, it’s a keeper.

This is the most critical factor of all, because it’s measured four times after dividing each horn length into quarters. Good mass tends to make horn length and prongs seem shorter than they really are. Circumference is difficult to judge, even for veteran antelope hunters. Complicating things, some animals have partially flattened bases, making them score lower than antelope with more rounded bases. Basal circumference on outstanding bucks will measure 6 to 7 inches.

The Solution: Study videos and books or visit a taxidermy shop to learn the art of ­judging mass. When you’re hunting, wait until the animal turns sideways to check whether the bases are round or flattened before making a circumference estimate.

Tip: Use an antelope’s eye ­circumference for comparison: It’s about 41â„2 inches. Also look at the diameter of the eye, about 11â„2 inches across. If the antelope’s bases look about an inch wider than the eye width, they’ll likely measure over 7 inches—now that’s a pronghorn.

A bunch of Grand Theft Auto games will be cheap on Steam until GTA 6 comes out, and only one sucks

In the run-up to GTA 6's November 19 launch, Rockstar has multiple classic Grand Theft Auto games on sale on Steam – and, nearest I can tell, only on Steam.

The Nintendo Switch eShop actually has a competing deal for the Grand Theft Auto Definitive Edition Trilogy, which is down 67% to $19.79, but that sale ends in 11 hours from the time of writing. On Steam, several GTA games are discounted until November 19 for an obviously timed special promotion.

On Steam, you'll find four games on sale via two actual products:

You can't argue with GTA 4 for $6, but you might argue with this version of, in particular, GTA 3.

The Definitive Edition Trilogy was famously panned at launch for wonky graphics and poor technical performance that, for the most part, has been patched up in the years since. However, the PC port for GTA 3's Definitive Edition is uniquely poorly rated for reasons that will sound familiar.

Here's how the three games in this trilogy compare in Steam reviews:

The reception to the two other games in the trilogy improved over time while GTA 3 steadily declined. Much like the day-one reviews, today's Steam players complain of Rockstar launcher interference, bugs, shoddy art, broken cinematics, and performance dips.

Weekly digests, tales from the communities you love, and more

Curiously, perhaps unrelated to the quality of the port, some of today's gamers also don't seem to take too kindly to GTA 3's design sensibilities, it being slightly older than Vice City and San Andreas, and certainly a rawer open-world experience.

Thomas Williamson, the CEO of the outsourced trilogy's original developer, Grove Street Games, said earlier this year that the criticism faced at launch was deserved.

"I agreed with most of the people's reactions," he said. "But unfortunately, I feel like we did not agree with how the game was released and the response to it from a development side. And I think that would have changed the narrative significantly."

However, he added, "at the end of the day, looking at the behind-the-scenes metrics on those games, there were a lot of people who were playing them and really enjoying them."

You won't find Grove Street Games listed on the store pages for these games anymore. Previously, Williamson called it a "dick move" for Rockstar to have Grove Street's name removed.

GTA 6 being pirated is just "a fact of life," says CEO of Rockstar owner, and lawyers are ready if it becomes a problem
